Playback that varies parameters with **Hold current view** refines every frame it calculates to the same view, so it, its export and the still draw the same curves. A camera flight is refined to each frame's view, in pieces (see [spatial camera paths](#spatial-camera-paths), **Refinement in pieces**); other animations that keep the study fixed draw the study as it stood. Without the setting, zoom stops at 8×; with it, at 1,000×. The wheel and a pinch zoom about the pointer, keeping the point of the plane through the middle of the drawing that lies under it in place.

**Checks.** Native tests (`engine3/view_test.go`) measure chords against closed forms: a trefoil knot through a window 0.06 wide, its chords in view and in the margin within 1.5 view tolerances and shorter than a quarter of the half diagonal, the rest within the study's tolerance, where the study's refinement alone strays more than three view tolerances, and nothing else in the result changed; a thin helix running 20 units toward the eye, every chord in view within 1.5 tolerances at its own depth, the farthest turns still straying more than three of the nearest tolerances, and a straight line cut to a quarter of the half diagonal near the view's plane and left whole ten units out; views behind the eye, nearer than the near distance, or no finer than the study, giving the study's result to the byte; a gap narrower than a sample step broken once, in the same interval, with views on it and beside it; every refined curve of a framed ribbon with strands and its surface, a canal, an involute family and an inversion refined about one of its own points; boxes placed by hand against each side of the margin, grown either side of meeting it, and the depth of pieces ahead of, across and behind the eye; and views that are not a frame of a page refused. In the browser (`tests/spatial-view-refinement.spec.ts`), **A spring folded into a cusp** opens 70× closer, and in its exported line drawing every chord of the curve lies within 1.5 view tolerances of the exact curve projected through the drawn camera; turning the setting off zooms out to 8× about the middle; keys stop at 8× and 1,000×; the wheel keeps the point under the pointer; and paused playback held at the view draws the still's pixels at progress 0.5 and 1.

### Surfaces along the curve

**Refine the surface too** (`adaptiveSurface`, with refinement on) adds columns to the surface a construction draws: a tangent developable, a framed ribbon with a width, a ruled surface or a canal. A column is a position between samples, in sample steps, where a curve that bounds the surface or lies on it drew a point: for the developable the curve and its edges `r ± LT`; for the ribbon the curve, its strands and its edges `r ± wD`; for the ruled surface the curve and its partner thread; for the canal the curve, its meridians and four seams at the mesh's angles 0, π/2, π and 3π/2. The edges and seams are refined for this alone, each to 1/5000 of the radius fitted to its own samples, and the edges are then drawn refined. Refinement bisects, so every column lies k/2ᵈ of a step past its sample, with d at most 10. Columns are taken a depth at a time, from the coarsest, while the whole depth fits within 2¹⁷ (131,072) vertices added to the mesh, at 4 a column for the developable's two sheets, 6 for the ribbon's two unshared triangles, 5 for a ruled surface's rulings and 25 for a canal's rings; a depth left out is reported (`capped`), so a surface that runs out is still refined evenly. Each column is placed by the construction's own pointwise evaluator, as a sample is: the developable's tangent and binormal at t, the ribbon's edges from the frame carried to t (see **Strands between samples**), the ruling from a(t) to b(φ(t)) with its normal S_t × S_u, and the contact circle at t (see **Meridians between samples**), its frame turned by the same spread holonomy and correction as the samples' rings and its normals (p − c(t))/R(t). So every point of a refined thread, edge or seam is a vertex of the mesh, and a refined meridian lies within a segment's sagitta, R(1 − cos(π/24)), of it. A column is left out where the evaluator has no point, where a developable's binormal is undefined or turns against either sample's, or where the contact circle is not real. A refined ribbon's cell is shaded by one normal, the cross product of its diagonals, so its two triangles shade alike. Columns lie only in intervals the surface joins. A gap or jump that an edge finds between samples leaves that interval's faces out; one a seam finds breaks the canal there, as a meridian's does, and the meridians are refined again with it. Each added triangle keeps the sample of its interval, so a reveal keeps or drops it with the interval. Rulings, cross-lines, contact circles, folds, the probe and framing keep the evenly spaced samples, and without the setting no surface changes.

**Checks.** Native tests check that the setting changes nothing but the mesh, the column count and the refined edges, and nothing at all without refinement; that columns are taken a whole depth at a time within their budget and never in a broken interval; that a ring run thirty times as fast as a ring below it carries every refined point of its thread on the mesh, every vertex on its closed-form ruling and the mesh's edge within 1.5 tolerances of the thread, where the uniform edge strays by more than four; that a tube of radius 0.1 round a ring coiled forty times, six samples a coil, has every vertex on the contact circle at its own parameter, 0.1 from the curve in its normal plane, and its refined meridians within a segment's sagitta of the mesh, where the uniform mesh leaves them ten sagittas off; that a tube round the trefoil still closes, its first and last rings unchanged, with its seam shown or spread, its neighboring rings turning together, and, untwisted and spread, every refined point of its first meridian a vertex of the mesh; that a canal without meridians finds a lost envelope with its seams alone and draws no face across it; that a ribbon twisted thirty times round a circle has every vertex on its closed-form edges, refined edges within 1.5 tolerances of them, where the uniform edges stray by more than four, and both triangles of every cell shaded alike; that a helix's tangent developable has every vertex on the tangent at its own parameter, the refined helix on the mesh and its far edges within 1.5 tolerances, where the uniform edges stray by more than four; that a gap a thread's refinement finds draws no face; that a developable's and a ribbon's edges, refined, find the jump a corner between samples makes in them, which the curve's own refinement joins, and leave no face across it; that a ruled surface's columns are shaded by its closed-form normal; and that every refined mesh is indexed and keeps each triangle within its own interval. In the browser, refining a ruled surface changes its shaded drawing but not its line drawing, unchecking the setting restores the drawing pixel for pixel and drops the field from the link, the control appears only with refinement on a construction that draws a surface, the twisted ribbon's exported edges carry their refined points, and the spring of tubing opens with 3,600 columns, four bisections of every interval.

## Spatial scale and translation

A study moved by a similarity `p ↦ s p + T` should be the same study, moved. Native tests (`engine3/similarity_test.go`) copy every spatial preset four ways: _tiny_ (s = 2⁻¹⁶), _tinier_ (s = 2⁻⁴⁰, curves only), _huge_ (s = 2¹⁰) and _far_ (T = (5000, −3000, 4000), thousands of times the presets' size). Each copy is computed with every diagnostic, and with the curve probe between samples where the curve allows it. Two studies no preset is are copied too: a coiled helix's tangent feet refined between samples, and a line inverted about a point it passes through. Every number of each result is compared with the original's, mapped:

- positions by `s p + T`, and lengths by s;
- curvatures by 1/s, and fluxes and irradiances by their dimensions;
- directions, angles, parameters and times unchanged, with principal directions compared up to sign;
- counts, flags, gaps and breaks exactly, except for the identity of a simultaneous capture as described below.

The copies stay within the inputs' limits (coordinates within ±10⁵, widths of at least 10⁻⁶), with these adjustments:

- A torus knot has no center and its radii are confined to 0.1–20, so the original and its copies are its four-term harmonic form.
- The tangent reach (at most 20) and a field's escape radius (at most 10⁵) are shortened in both studies, so the copy's stay within them.
- Surfaces and mirrors are placed at the origin, so they are only scaled. Their domains and a level set's box are at least 10⁻⁶ wide, so only curves are made tinier.

**Units.** The floors below which a curve stands still, jumps, or has an unstable derivative are measured in the curve's own units. Its size L is twice the robustly fitted radius of its points at 65 evenly spaced parameters and 64 probes staggered by the golden fraction, unchanged by a translation. The fit trims isolated asymptotic extremes, so a rounded value at a pole cannot set every regular branch's floors when scaling brings it below the coordinate guard. The staggered probes keep a periodic curve that aliases the uniform grid from inheriting absolute floors; this bounded estimate does not certify arbitrary expressions against aliasing. Over its parameter span Δ:

- A velocity v stands still where `|v| < 10⁻⁹ L/Δ`, for the base curve's L. A curve derived from it (an input, an involute, a coil, a frame) is measured by the base's units too.
- A numerical velocity is stable where it agrees with the half-step one within 10⁻³ of its size plus `10⁻⁸ L/Δ`.
- An expression curve or differenced input has a stable acceleration where it agrees within 10⁻² plus `10⁻⁴ max(L/Δ², |v|/Δ)`.
- A chord jumps where it exceeds `8 |v_mid| h + 10⁻⁷ L`, for sample step h.
- A projection's or involute member's image collapses to a point where its points lie within `10⁻⁹ (L + d)` of the first, for d the distance from the pole or the string length. An inversion's image does so within `10⁻⁹ (R + d)`, for the inversion radius R.

A derived input differenced numerically uses its own size for its derivatives, or the base's when it has none. A curve has no size of its own when its points are apart by no more than 10⁻¹² of their distance from the origin. The canal's profile ρ is a pure number, and keeps absolute floors, as does a curve with no size of its own. Framing's radius is the family's own, with 10⁻⁴ kept only for a lone point. Its Tukey fences apply where the quartiles' spread exceeds 10⁻¹² of the family's largest quartile magnitude, the rounding its coordinates carry. Every preset's result is unchanged by these floors.

**Error budgets.** A difference is measured relative to the number's own size plus the study's unit for its kind: `s R` for a length, R being the original's bounding radius, and `1/(s R)` for a curvature. For a position, the size is its distance from the moved origin plus `|T|`.

- **Scaled copies.** Scaling by a power of two is exact in every homogeneous operation, so every number agrees within 10⁻¹³ (at most 10⁻¹⁴ measured), and every decision is the same. These remaining limits can change the decisions:
  - A numerically differenced r‴ (torsion of an expression curve, a trajectory, a chase or a derived input) is stable by a floor of `10⁻⁴ max(1, |v|/Δ²)`. A tiny copy finds torsions the original cannot, and with them the normals of a framed ribbon's surface probe.
  - A framed ribbon's surface probe tests its tangent plane against the larger of `S_t`, a length per parameter, and the unit offset direction `S_u`. A tinier copy finds no normals there.
  - A seed is reported as resting where the field's speed is below 10⁻⁹, as the notebook's note states, so a tinier copy's seeds are marked resting. The trajectory evaluator's acceleration-stability floor, `10⁻⁴ max(1, |v|/Δ)`, also remains absolute; these preset copies do not expose a failure from it.
- **Far copies of evaluated geometry.** A knot, a harmonic curve or a level surface keeps the shift's rounding, about 10⁻¹² of a study a few units across. Positions and lengths agree within 10⁻¹¹ (2·10⁻¹³ measured), and directions and curvatures within 10⁻⁹ (4·10⁻¹¹).
- **Far copies of differenced curves.** An expression curve, a derived input or a ruled surface's second thread is differenced from positions rounded at `|T|`. Each derivative divides that rounding by another power of the stencil step. Positions and lengths agree within 10⁻⁶ (8·10⁻⁸ measured), and tangents and other directions within 10⁻⁴ (3·10⁻⁶). Their second and third derivatives are poorly conditioned where the curve slows. So curvature, torsion, Frenet frames, a developable's mesh normals, and which samples have them, are compared only at scale. A curve-built surface's probe is compared only at scale too, since it is always differenced.
- **Far trajectories and chases.** These are integrated to a tolerance relative to the distance from the origin (10⁻¹⁰, and 10⁻¹² for a chase), so a far copy takes coarser and fewer steps. Its points agree within 10⁻⁴ (1.5·10⁻⁵ measured, on Rössler's band). In a symmetric chase several adjacent pairs can capture simultaneously, and rounding can change which pair is reported first. A different reported pair is accepted only when both pairs' actual gaps in both returned final states are within 10⁻⁶ of the capture distance, relative to that distance; A capture whose alternate pair falls outside this gap budget keeps its identity. Lorenz's attractor is not compared far from the origin: its trajectories separate exponentially, so any change in rounding grows to the attractor's size within the interval.
- **Far level surfaces.** The moved grid samples F at points rounded differently. Where the original's F is exactly zero at a grid point or a box corner (the gyroid through `(±π, ±π, ±π)`, a torus through grid points), the copy is triangulated differently. It is compared by its geometry instead: every original vertex and section point lies within 10⁻² of a cell of the copy's (3·10⁻³ measured), and every point of the copy within 10⁻⁹ of a cell of the original's level set (3·10⁻¹¹ measured).

**Checks.** Each of the following focused tests failed before its fix, and passes after it:

- the regular branches and pole gaps of `tan(t)` on [−π, π] survived all tested scales and translations; enormous rounded pole values entering the coordinate guard after shrinking had erased the regular branches;
- a 64-turn circle, in harmonic and expression form, retained every sample and break at s = 2⁻⁴⁰; the original uniform size grid saw a constant point and the scaled curve lost every sample;
- framing's radius scaled with its points, which a floor of 10⁻⁴ held fixed;
- a family whose points share a coordinate up to rounding kept its bounds when moved, which the fences' absolute 10⁻¹² threshold trimmed;
- the cusp of the cardioid traced by a circle's tangent feet stayed a gap at s = 2⁻⁴⁰ and 2¹⁶, against an absolute speed floor of 10⁻⁹;
- the step of `|t − 1|/(t − 1)` stayed a break at 2⁻⁴⁰, against an absolute chord floor of 10⁻⁷;
- an endpoint cusp, differenced one-sidedly, stayed a gap far from the origin, against an absolute stability floor of 10⁻⁸;
- the unstable acceleration of `|t|^1.5` at t = 0 stayed unknown at 2⁻⁴⁰.

The tinier copies found the collapse tests' absolute floors, which made every tinier inversion, projection and involute member a point. Before these fixes, 90 of the 107 tiny copies, all 72 tinier ones, one huge copy and two far copies failed. Reverting any one of the new floors, alone, makes a test fail again, at each place it is used.

**Drawing.** The drawing holds its positions in single precision, about 6·10⁻⁸ of a coordinate's size, so a study far from the origin would lose its detail before the camera subtracts its center. Positions are therefore measured from a _drawing origin_ before they become single precision: the bounds' center, once that lies more than 8 radii from the origin. Each result has its own, and the probe and the cut's edge use their result's. The camera's center and the cut's planes are moved to that origin in double precision (`p − o`, `d − n̂·o`), so the GPU, Lines (SVG), the cut's edge, the peel's range and the loop check all work in numbers about the study's size. Arc lengths, joints and tapers depend only on differences, and near and far clipping on the framing radius, so none of them changes. A study within 8 radii keeps world coordinates, at most about 5·10⁻⁷ of its radius from where double precision puts them, as every preset always has; their drawings are unchanged bit for bit.

Browser tests (`tests/spatial-rebase.spec.ts`) compare a study built by hand with its copy moved by (5000, −3000, 4000). Its lines, orthographic, through a lens and zoomed in, with the cut and with occlusion, lie within 10⁻³ px of the original's on a 2000 px page (4·10⁻⁴ measured), against 0.2–3 px before. The cut's edge, a level set and the peel's range agree within 10⁻⁶ after moving by T, and the loop check sees a step of 10⁻³ radius, which it took for none before. The live canvas of the ribbon staircase, against its copy scaled by 2⁻⁶ and moved by the same T, differs in no pixel by more than 48 levels, where 7% of its inked pixels did before.

## Spatial harmonic curves

A spatial harmonic curve is `r(t) = c₀ + Σ[Aₖ cos(ωₖt) + Bₖ sin(ωₖt)]` with 1–8 terms, each a frequency ωₖ (radians per unit t, within ±1000) and two 3D vectors Aₖ and Bₖ (coordinates within ±100000), about a center c₀ (within ±100000). Term k is a vector turning around the ellipse spanned by Aₖ and Bₖ, from Aₖ at t = 0 toward Bₖ; parallel vectors flatten it to a segment, and a negative frequency turns it the other way. A zero frequency makes the term the fixed translation Aₖ. Position and both derivatives are exact: each term adds `ωₖ(−Aₖ sin ωₖt + Bₖ cos ωₖt)` to r′ and `−ωₖ²(Aₖ cos ωₖt + Bₖ sin ωₖt)` to r″, so the developable's normal guard is scaled by `Σ ωₖ²(|Aₖ| + |Bₖ|)`, a bound on |r″|, instead of a stencil's noise floor. A planar Fourier term ρe^{i(kt+φ)} is the special case `A = ρ(cos φ, sin φ, 0)`, `B = ρ(−sin φ, cos φ, 0)`.

**Closure policy.** The domain is always the entered `[t from, to]`; the curve is never forced closed. A term _moves_ the curve when its frequency and at least one of its vectors are nonzero. The period of the moving frequencies is found exactly as for [planar harmonic curves](#harmonic-curves), by the shared `engine/closure` package: 2π/gcd for whole numbers, continued fractions with denominators up to 1000 otherwise, and no period (0) for incommensurate frequencies such as 1 and φ or periods longer than 100000. The curve is _closed_ when the domain spans a whole number m ≥ 1 of periods (`|span/T − m| ≤ 10⁻⁹·span/T`); then its last sample is its first, so the seam has no roundoff gap. Otherwise it is drawn as an open arc, and the interface offers to trace one full period from the domain start. When nothing moves, the curve is a single point and is refused with a message rather than sampled into gaps.

**Term identity.** Terms keep their input order, which is their identity: a zero term is neither dropped nor reordered, its vector starts and ends at the same joint, and animation tracks name terms by number. At each representative sample (the construction's line count), `Joints[k]` is where term k's vector starts (`Joints[0] = c₀`) and the chain ends at the curve point. The vector sums at every representative sample, and each turning term's ellipse around its joint at the last one shown, are construction lines. Framing fits each term's joints and its ellipse's axis extents (`±√(Aᵢ² + Bᵢ²)` along each axis i) as separate families, so a large, slow vector nearly canceled by another stays in view even when the curve is small. The harmonic geometry is reported under every construction; the construction still acts on the curve alone.

**Checks.** Native tests verify a single-term ellipse about a moved center (every sample on the ellipse and in its plane, exactly `c₀ + A cos t + B sin t`), exact derivatives against central differences including a zero-frequency translation, the planar reduction against the complex Fourier sum, closure for whole, rational and commensurate irrational frequencies over one period, two periods and part of one, an incommensurate open arc that is not closed, a zero term that leaves the curve and period unchanged while keeping its place, translation of the curve, vectors, and framing by c₀, framing of a nearly canceled large vector, every construction on a harmonic curve, and validation of term counts, frequencies, vectors, center, domain, and a curve that does not move. The WebAssembly bridge test checks an ellipse, its closure and representative positions, an open arc, and the refusal.

## Spatial frames, offsets, and ribbons

A space curve's tangent T leaves a whole plane of normal directions, so any offset or ribbon must first choose a _frame_ (T, U, V): unit vectors with U ⊥ T and V = T × U. The framed construction offers two.

**Rotation-minimizing frame** (the default for drawing). U is carried along the curve by parallel transport: it changes only in the tangent direction, `U′ = −(U · T′) T`, so it never turns about T. Between consecutive samples it is carried by the _double reflection_ method of Wang, Jüttler, Zheng and Liu: reflect U and T in the plane bisecting the chord rᵢrᵢ₊₁, then reflect in the plane taking the reflected tangent to Tᵢ₊₁; the result is projected onto the new normal plane only to remove roundoff. Each step is a rotation, so orthonormality holds to roundoff, and a start rotated by φ stays rotated by exactly φ. It is fourth-order accurate: on the helix `(2 cos t, 2 sin t, t/2)` the error against the exact rotation-minimizing normal `cos φ N + sin φ B`, `φ = −τs`, falls from 1.8·10⁻⁹ to 1.1·10⁻¹⁰ to 6.9·10⁻¹² as the samples double from 240 to 960. Hanson and Ma's parallel-transport report describes the same frame by per-step rotations.

**Initial normal and breaks.** The reference normal N₀ is a world vector. Where each unbroken stretch of the curve begins (the domain start and after every invalid sample or interval break), U starts as N₀ projected onto that normal plane. If N₀ is within 10⁻⁶ of tangent there, the coordinate axis least aligned with T is projected instead, and the stretch is counted as a fallback. The frame is never transported across a break: it restarts, and the number of stretches is reported. Changing N₀ turns the whole frame of a stretch by one constant angle about T.

**Frenet frame** (a diagnostic). U is the principal normal N and V the binormal B = (r′ × r″)/|r′ × r″|. It is undefined where the curvature vanishes, by the same normal guard as the developable, and those samples are counted and left as gaps. It reverses at an inflection; an interval across which B reverses is broken rather than joined (a _flip_). A straight segment or a regular inflection therefore breaks a Frenet ribbon but not a transported one.

**Offset direction, twist, ribbon, and strands.** Arc length s is Simpson's rule per sample interval and is not carried across a break; L is the total. The offset direction is `D = cos θ U + sin θ V` with `θ(s) = θ₀ + 2π N s / L`: θ₀ is in radians from U toward V, and the twist N counts turns of D about T over the drawn curve. Because θ depends on arc length, a regular reparameterization leaves the drawing unchanged. The ribbon is `S(s, u) = r(s) + u D(s)` for `|u| ≤ w`; each of m offset strands is `r + d D` with D turned by a further 2πk/m. These are framed constructions, not tangent developables: a ribbon is flat only in special cases, and a strand whose offset exceeds the local radius of curvature can cusp or cross itself. The ribbon's shading uses each drawn triangle's own normal, and its cross-lines join `r ± wD` at the representative samples. Frame twist is geometry; rotating the camera turns only the view.

**Closed loops and the seam.** On an unbroken closed curve (a torus knot, or a harmonic curve over whole periods), the transported U returns rotated about T₀ by the _holonomy_ α, the signed angle from its start, reported in (−π, π]. For a curve with nonzero curvature, `α ≡ −∫τ ds (mod 2π)`: it belongs to the curve and does not depend on N₀. It is never hidden. **Show the seam** (the default) leaves the transported frame as it is, so the ribbon and strands visibly fail to meet at the start, where arms and an arc mark the angle between the start and returned offset directions. **Distribute the correction** turns the frame by `−α s / L`, a uniform extra twist reported as the correction, so it closes. The ribbon then closes when the twist N is a whole number of turns; otherwise the seam reports the remaining angle. A Frenet frame is periodic on a closed loop and has no holonomy.

**Checks.** Native tests verify orthonormal right-handed frames, fourth-order convergence to the helix's exact transported normal, exact prescribed twist on a circle (strands and ribbon edges to 10⁻¹²), strands evenly spaced at distance d, continuity with a single stretch through a straight run and a regular inflection (where the Frenet frame is undefined or flips and is broken), a changed N₀ rotating every frame by one angle with the holonomy unchanged, holonomy against the independently integrated total torsion of a closed harmonic loop, the exposed seam, a distributed correction proportional to arc length that closes the ribbon, a half turn of twist that leaves a π seam, restarts after a pole, the tangent-reference fallback, rigid motion and reparameterization, ribbon triangles and cross-lines, strand framing, and validation. The WebAssembly bridge test checks a twisted circle ribbon, its seam, and refusals.

## Spatial curvature, torsion and the parameter probe

The parameter probe describes the base curve at one sample, and its plots describe it at every sample. With r′, r″ and r‴ the derivatives with respect to the parameter t:

- curvature κ = |r′ × r″| / |r′|³, and radius of curvature 1/κ;
- binormal B = (r′ × r″)/|r′ × r″|, principal normal N = B × T, tangent T = r′/|r′|, so (T, N, B) is right-handed and orthonormal;
- torsion τ = (r′ × r″)·r‴ / |r′ × r″|². With this sign a right-handed helix `(R cos t, R sin t, ht)`, h > 0, has τ = h/(R² + h²) > 0, and its mirror image the opposite;
- osculating center r + N/κ, the center of the circle of radius 1/κ in the plane of T and N that best fits the curve there.

κ and τ do not depend on the parameterization or on rigid motions. The frame is the Frenet frame of **Framed ribbon · Frenet**, from the same guard: it is defined where |r′ × r″| exceeds 10⁻⁸ (analytic knots and harmonic sums) or 10⁻⁶ (expressions and integrated paths, whose r″ is numerical) times |r′| times the curve's scale of r″. Below that the sample is **flat**: κ is reported as 0, and N, B, τ and the osculating circle are undefined and not drawn, as at an inflection or along a straight stretch. Where r″ itself is unknown (the evaluator discards an unstable numerical second derivative) κ is unknown too. An osculating radius beyond 100 study radii puts the center at infinity: the radius is still reported, but the circle is not drawn. A closed curve's last sample repeats its first and is counted once.

**Third derivative.** A torus knot and a harmonic sum have r‴ analytically. For expressions, field trajectories and pursuit paths, r‴ is a central difference of the curve's own r″ over steps of 10⁻³ and 5·10⁻⁴ of the domain's width, second-order accurate, one-sided over three points near the domain's ends or where r″ stops being known (a trajectory's early end). Where the two steps disagree by more than 1% (plus a small floor), τ is reported as unknown rather than guessed, as at a jump in r‴. On the twisted cubic `(t, t², t³)`, whose κ = 2√(9t⁴ + 9t² + 1)/(1 + 4t² + 9t⁴)^{3/2} and τ = 3/(9t⁴ + 9t² + 1), the largest relative errors over [−1.5, 1.5] are 2·10⁻⁹ in κ and 9·10⁻⁶ in τ; on the example helix they are 10⁻¹⁰ and 3·10⁻⁵, and on the rising vortex's integrated helix τ is within 3·10⁻⁶. The readout therefore gives four significant figures. Near a flat sample τ divides by a vanishing |r′ × r″|², so its plot trims values beyond outer Tukey fences (three interquartile ranges past the quartiles), pins them to the edge, and counts them.

Unless it stands between samples (below), the probe moves between samples, so its t is always tᵢ = min + (max − min)·i/n; more samples give finer steps. Moving the probe along the curve as an animation shows sample round(p·n) at progress p, so it starts at t = min and ends at t = max exactly.

**Between samples.** With **At any t, between samples** (`probe.between`), the curve probe stands at the parameter `t = min·(1 − u) + max·u` for its place u in the domain, and Go describes the curve there (`engine3/probe.go`, asked by `probe`, a parameter or a share of the length). The curve is the one the construction acts on, as at the samples: the base, or its derived input. Its point, frame, κ, τ and osculating center come from the function the per-sample diagnostics use (`describe`), with the same binormal guard, r‴ and clipping at 100 study radii, so at a sample the probe reads exactly what the sample does; at the end of a closed curve's domain it reads the first sample, as the last sample repeats it. The arc length is the length at the last sample at or before t, continued over the rest of the way by Simpson's rule on the speed at its ends and midpoint. The highlighted construction at t comes from the evaluators refinement uses (see [Refinement between samples](#refinement-between-samples)): the tangent ruling r ∓ L·T, the tangent foot and image, the inversion's source and image, the framed ribbon's cross-line from the frame carried one transport step from the sample before (with its twist and any distributed correction carried by arc length), the ruled surface's partner b(φ(t)), each involute filament with its string length carried on from the sample before, and a harmonic curve's chain of turning vectors at t. Each is absent where a sample there would have none: across a break the frame joins, outside the partner's domain, or where a filament is unreached. A canal has no highlight there either. A share s of the length places it where the drawn length is s times the whole: at a sample whose length is exactly that, and otherwise by bisection within the drawn interval that passes it. A field trajectory or pursuit, integrated step by step, has no evaluator at an arbitrary t and keeps snapping to its samples; a surface, mirror or level set has no curve probe. Over a drawn study the notebook asks Go for the probe alone (`ProbeOnly`), which leaves the study's result untouched; the drawing is busy until it arrives. Go keeps the last study it computed for the probe, refinement included, so its breaks and lengths are the drawing's, and a move over the same study evaluates one point; the notebook keeps one such request in flight and asks for the latest place when it returns, so a drag never queues work. Moving it along the curve as an animation places it at `t = min·(1 − p) + max·p` at progress p. While the parameters vary, Go places it with each frame: at its t (absent where the frame's domain leaves it out), at the same share of the frame's drawn length, or at the frame's own share of the domain, so the held point no longer steps by up to half a sample spacing. A reveal draws it once it has drawn the sample after it. The live drawing records where it stands (`data-probe-t`, and `data-probe-sample` when on a sample), and a still export records `{ t }` without a sample index.

**Arc length.** With the probe on, the diagnostics also carry the drawn curve's arc length from its first sample to each sample, by Simpson's rule on each interval from the speeds |r′| at its ends and midpoint (the same values that decide its breaks). It is null where the curve is not drawn, and an interval across a break or an undefined sample adds nothing, so it measures the drawn pieces only; a closed curve's last sample stands at its whole length. On a helix, whose speed is constant, it is exact to rounding; on `(cos t, sin t, t²/2)` over [0, 3], whose length is (u√(1+u²) + asinh u)/2, the error falls by more than 12 from 240 to 480 samples, and the length is unchanged by a regular reparameterization to 10⁻⁶.

**The probe while parameters vary.** Each frame of a parameter animation is computed afresh, with the diagnostics the probe needs, and **Probe** chooses where it stands on that frame. Let i₀ be the probe's own sample, on the study as playback began, and p the progress. **Stays at its t** takes t₀ = min₀ + (max₀ − min₀)·i₀/n₀ and stands at the frame's nearest sample, round((t₀ − min)/(max − min)·n); if t₀ lies more than half a step outside the frame's domain, the frame has no probe and the panel says so, rather than clamping it to an end. **Keeps its share of the length** takes σ = s(i₀)/S, the share of the drawn length at its own sample, and stands at the drawn sample whose length is nearest σS on each frame (the first on a tie); a probe on an undrawn sample has no share and draws nothing. **Moves along the curve** stands at round(p·n), as the probe's own animation does, so the probe and the parameters move together. Each frame's readout and plots describe that frame; nothing is interpolated between frames, so the frame flipping across an inflection is drawn as it is, never blended, and a flat sample is marked flat. Snapping to samples moves the held point by up to half a sample's spacing between frames; a probe between samples (above) does not snap. On a grid (a surface, a mirror or its light) **Stays at its row and column** stands at the same fractions of each frame's rows and columns; **Stays at its u and v** (`point`; on a curve's surface its t and its place across) takes the parameters (u₀, v₀) of its own row and column on the study as playback began and stands at the frame's row and column nearest them, each by its own parameter values, so a reversed or uneven grid is read as it is; like the curve probe's t, a parameter more than half the end's step outside the frame's range leaves that frame without a probe and the panel names it, while a canal's turn around its contact circle wraps; and **Moves along** stands at row round(p·m). Holding (u₀, v₀) snaps to each frame's grid, as staying at t snaps to its samples: Go evaluates a surface only on its grid. Framing (the hold, follow and fit cameras) uses the study's bounds alone, never the probe's circle, which may leave the view as its radius grows.

**The probe in fixed animations.** In both notebooks, every animation that keeps the study fixed draws the probe while it is on: in 2D, drawing along the curve and tracing light; in 3D, revealing, orbiting, flying a camera path, tracing light and peeling with the cut (the cut never hides the probe). The probe stands at the user's own step i₀, the curve sample or the surface's row and column, and moving it waits until the animation stops. A reveal at progress p draws samples 0…⌊p·n⌋, so it draws the probe once ⌊p·n⌋ reaches the sample that step describes; until then the frame has no probe and the panel says the drawing has not reached it. A curve probe's step is its sample. A surface probe's row r is the sample its row is drawn with: row r on a patch or a mirror, whose reveal runs along u, and base sample `along[r]` on a canal, tangent developable, framed ribbon or ruled surface, whose rows are drawn with the curve (`revealedProbe` in `probe.ts`). Playback and exports draw each frame's probe alike, and the live readout keeps describing the animation, not the probe, which does not move.

On **A trefoil's osculating circle, all the way round**, `(sin t + 2 sin 2t, cos t − 2 cos 2t, −sin 3t)` at t = π has r′ = (3, 0, 3), r″ = (0, 9, 0) and r‴ = (−15, 0, −27), so T = (1, 0, 1)/√2, N = (0, 1, 0), B = (−1, 0, 1)/√2, κ = 1/2 and τ = −2/9. N is the camera's turntable axis, so a level turn holds it in every view: looking along B (yaw π/4) the osculating circle is seen face on, and along ∓T (yaw 3π/4 and −π/4) edge on, as the segment of length 2/κ = 4 from the point along N. A browser test checks the readout and that the circle's ink shrinks edge on.

On **A helix pressed through its circle**, `(cos t, at, sin t)` has r′ × r″ = (−a sin t, −1, a cos t) and r‴ = (sin t, 0, −cos t), so κ = 1/(1 + a²) and τ = −a/(1 + a²) everywhere: at a = 0 the coils press into one circle, which is its own osculating circle, and τ changes sign with the hand. On **A twisted cubic losing its bend**, `(t, at², t³)` has r′ = (1, 0, 0), r″ = (0, 2a, 0) and r‴ = (0, 0, 6) at t = 0, so κ = 2|a| and τ = 3/a there: as a → 0 the torsion at the middle diverges while elsewhere it falls toward 0, N turns from +y to −y, and at a = 0 the middle is flat. Browser tests read these values from the probe at the presets' frames. On **A knot reparameterized in place**, s = t + ½ sin t sin a has ds/dt ≥ ½ and fixes s at 0 and 2π, so every frame draws the same trefoil; keeping its share of the length, the probe's κ and τ hold to within the snapping (about 1% there) while its t wanders, and staying at its t, it slides along the knot. The highlighted construction at the sample is the study's own geometry at that index (the tangent ruling, each filament's unwinding string, the tangent, perpendicular and foot or reflected pole, the correspondence segment, the ribbon's cross-line, the ruling, or a pursuit's polygon), not a recomputation. A harmonic curve, under any construction, also highlights its chain of generating vectors at the sample's time, each turning term with its ellipse around its joint, as the drawing shows them at the last representative sample; Go returns the chain at every sample (`harmonic.chains`) only with the probe's diagnostics, so studies without the probe are unchanged. A vector field with more than one seed highlights its timeline: every trajectory's point at the sample's time, joined in seed order from the study's own paths, open, and broken where a trajectory has ended, the image of the polygon through the seeds under the flow.

On **Epicycles drawing a cinquefoil**, the (2, 5) torus knot `(2 + 0.8 cos 5t)(cos 2t x̂ + sin 2t ẑ) + 0.8 sin 5t ŷ` is four turning vectors, since `cos 5t (cos 2t, sin 2t) = ½(cos 7t, sin 7t) + ½(cos 3t, −sin 3t)`: a circle of radius 2 at frequency 2, circles of radius 0.4 at 7 and −3, and an upright segment of half-length 0.8 at 5. At t = 0 every term stands at its A, so the chain runs straight along x in the ratio 5 : 1 : 1 to the point. On **A row of seeds wound up by a vortex**, `(x′, z′) = (−z, x)/(x² + z²)` with `y′ = a` turns a seed at radius ρ by t/ρ² while it rises a·t, so the timeline at time t lies in one plane on the spiral θ = t/ρ²; seeds spaced evenly in 1/ρ² keep neighbors an equal angle 0.04t apart. A browser test checks both in the exported page coordinates, where an orthographic drawing preserves ratios along a line and affine combinations.

On **An osculating circle gliding round Viviani's window**, Viviani's curve `(1 + cos t, sin t, 2 sin(t/2))`, where the sphere of radius 2 meets the cylinder of radius 1 through its center, has κ = √(13 + 3 cos t)/(3 + cos t)^{3/2} and τ = 6 cos(t/2)/(13 + 3 cos t), which change all the way round; with 240 samples the probe stands between them and glides. **A trefoil's perpendicular feet, swept at any t** slides the probe round the trefoil from a pole above its center, with its tangent, perpendicular and foot.

**Checks between samples.** Native tests (`engine3/probe_test.go`) verify that the probe at every tested sample, ends included, equals the sample's diagnostics bit for bit, and its construction the study's own points within rounding, for every highlighted construction on knots, helices, harmonic curves and both derived inputs; a helix's κ, τ, frame, center and arc length off the samples against closed forms; a knot's frame and curvature against independent derivatives and its arc length against an independent Gauss–Legendre quadrature; the tangent ruling, tangent foot, orthotomic, inversion of an orthotomic, involute filaments, Frenet ribbon, ruled partner and harmonic chain against closed forms off the samples; a twisted rotation-minimizing ribbon's cross-line converging on a study ten times as dense at its samples; the involute input of a helix, a planar circle involute with radius of curvature (a/w)|c − s| and no torsion; shares of a helix's length at those shares of the domain; a closed curve's end read as its start; a straight line flat and an undefined point empty; a far center clipped; that asking for the probe leaves the rest of the result byte-identical; the probe alone from the kept study against a fresh computation through changes of study; and the refusals of malformed queries, trajectories, pursuits and surfaces. The WASM bridge checks the probe alone against the full result's on a trefoil. Browser tests (`tests/spatial-probe-between.spec.ts`) check the readout against an elliptic helix's closed forms at a sample and between samples, probe-only requests and a coalesced drag, the export's record, every animation mode that draws the probe, and an exported video's frame against a still of the probe at its own t.

## Spatial principal curvatures and the surface probe

The surface probe describes a surface patch, a canal surface, or a tangent developable, framed ribbon or ruled surface (see **Ruled surfaces** below) at one point of a grid, and its plots describe the grid's lines through it (see **Plots** below). Conventions are those of the focal sheets: with the unit normal n and the shape operator A = −dn, the principal curvatures κ are A's eigenvalues and the principal directions its unit eigenvectors, so a sphere with its outward normal has κ = −1/R. Each principal curvature has a focal point, its **center**, x + n/κ on the normal line, and a normal-section circle of radius 1/|κ| through the point in the plane of n and that direction, centered there. The readout also gives the Gaussian curvature K = κ₁κ₂ and the mean curvature H = (κ₁ + κ₂)/2, which do not depend on the numbering. A center beyond 100 study radii is at infinity: its curvature is still reported, but neither center nor circle is drawn. Where the two curvatures agree the point is an umbilic and no direction is drawn; where the surface is singular there is no normal and nothing but the point is drawn. Where the tangent plane cannot be found from stable derivatives, as on a ruled surface where S_t needs an r″ or r‴ the evaluator discarded, there is no normal either, and nothing but the point is drawn, but the point is not singular: the readout says the tangent plane is unknown there and leaves its curvatures unknown, and the notes count such points apart from the singular ones and from those whose curvatures alone are unknown.

**Patches.** (A mirror or interface is probed as its patch; its light has a probe of its own, see [the light probe](#spatial-wavefronts-and-the-light-probe).) The grid is the patch's own, and the values are those its focal sheets are built from (see [focal sheets](#spatial-surfaces-normals-offsets-and-focal-sheets)): κ₁ ≥ κ₂, swapped together with the normal's sign when the normal is reversed. The probe and the focal sheets clip at the same reach, from the same evaluations.

**Offsets.** A patch with an offset d ≠ 0 offers **Describe → The offset** beside the patch. The offset X_d = X + dn (see [offsets](#spatial-surfaces-normals-offsets-and-focal-sheets)) is probed on the patch's own grid, from the same evaluations, and Go returns it in place of the patch's (`offsetDiagnostics`; asking for both is refused). It shares the patch's normal n, as drawn, and since dX_d = (I − dA) dX, its shape operator is A(I − dA)⁻¹: the same principal directions, with curvatures κᵢ/(1 − dκᵢ). Each center X_d + n(1 − dκᵢ)/κᵢ = X + n/κᵢ is therefore the patch's focal point: parallel surfaces share their focal sheets, and the offset's radii of curvature are the patch's less d. The branches keep the patch's numbers, so each is drawn in its focal sheet's ink and centered on it; where the offset has folded, (1 − dκ₁)(1 − dκ₂) < 0, κ₁ < κ₂ can hold, and the panel says the point has folded and counts such points. Where |1 − dκᵢ| ≤ 10⁻⁹(1 + |dκᵢ|) the offset meets that focal sheet in a cuspidal edge and is singular there; a chart singularity of the patch gives the offset no point. The normal line runs on to the patch's point X, marked with a cross. Centers are clipped at 100 study radii from the offset's point. Native tests check the torus (its offset is the torus of tube radius r + d, with curvatures −cos v/(R + (r + d) cos v) and −1/(r + d), folded past the core circle), the sphere (radius ρ + d, umbilic; collapsed and singular everywhere at d = −ρ), the cylinder (flat along its rulings, singular on its axis), and on an ellipsoid, a saddle and a monkey saddle with either normal that the offset's points are the drawn offset sheet's, its directions the patch's, and each center the patch's focal point to 10⁻⁹.

**Focal sheets.** Every patch offers **Describe → Focal sheet 1** and **Focal sheet 2**, the sheets F = X + n/κᵢ drawn by the [focal sheets](#spatial-surfaces-normals-offsets-and-focal-sheets) layers, probed on the patch's grid and returned in place of the patch's diagnostics (`focalDiagnostics`, 1 or 2; asking for it with the patch's or the offset's is refused). Along eᵢ the sheet moves only along n, dF = d(1/κᵢ) n, and across it along n and the other direction, so its tangent plane holds n and its normal is eᵢ: the patch's normal line touches the sheet at F. The sheet's second fundamental form is −dF·d eᵢ, which needs how κᵢ and eᵢ change, and so the patch's third derivatives, which every patch supplies exactly. In the parameters, with first and second fundamental forms g and b, eᵢ = X_u w¹ + X_v w² where b w = κg w and wᵀg w = 1; along each parameter κ′ = wᵀ(b′ − κg′)w and w′ = c w̄ − ½(wᵀg′w) w with c = w̄ᵀ(b′ − κg′)w/(κ − κ̄), where w̄ is the other branch's eigenvector, b′ = X_ij′·n + X_ij·n′ and n′ = −dX(g⁻¹b). Then F′ = X′ + n′/κ − κ′n/κ². The sheet's normal is oriented continuously along u, from the sample before it at the same v, or along v on the first row, so the plots along u run on, and so do those along v unless an umbilic of the patch lies between the first row and the probe's; no orientation suits a whole grid around an umbilic, where the principal directions turn by half a turn. Its curvatures, numbered κ₁ ≥ κ₂, are taken with that normal, so their signs and numbers can swap where it does. A sample has no point where the patch has no normal or κᵢ puts the center beyond 100 study radii. It is singular at an umbilic of the patch, where eᵢ is undefined and the two sheets meet, and where |F_u × F_v| ≤ 10⁻⁹ max(|F_u|, |F_v|)²: on a ridge, where κᵢ is stationary along eᵢ and the sheet has a cuspidal edge, and wherever the whole sheet is a curve or a point, as a surface of revolution's sheet of parallels is its axis. The drawing marks the patch's point X with a cross and joins it to F along the patch's normal. The sheet's own centers, clipped at 100 study radii, lie on the focal sheets of the sheet. Native tests check that the third derivatives match differences of the second; that an oblate and a prolate spheroid's meridian sheet is its meridian's evolute spun about the axis, with that surface of revolution's K and |H|, one center on the axis and the other the evolute's own, singular over the equator and pointless at the poles, while the parallels' sheet, the axis, is singular throughout; that an elliptic cylinder's sheet is the cylinder over the ellipse's evolute, flat along its rulings and as curved as the evolute across them, singular at its four cusps, the other sheet at infinity; that the torus's and the sphere's sheets are singular everywhere; and, on a triaxial ellipsoid with either normal, two paraboloids and a monkey saddle, that Richardson-extrapolated second differences of the sheets' own points, from the patch's second derivatives alone, converge on the same K and |H| to 10⁻⁶ and the same principal directions away from the cuspidal edges.

**Between grid samples.** With **At any u and v, between samples** (`probe.betweenGrid`), a patch's probe, on the patch, its offset or either focal sheet, stands at `u = uMin·(1 − p) + uMax·p` and `v = vMin·(1 − q) + vMax·q` for its places p and q in the domain, and Go describes it exactly there (`surfaceProbe`, engine3/surfacepoint.go): the patch is analytic, so its derivatives at any (u, v) are exact, and the point is described by the function that describes each grid sample, so at a sample it reads that sample to the bit. A point outside the domain is refused. A focal sheet's normal, eᵢ up to sign, takes the side of the nearest grid sample's normal (or, where that sample has none, of the one before it along u, then along v), so the probe keeps one side as it passes between samples. Umbilics, cuspidal edges and centers at infinity are judged by the same tolerances as on the grid, against the patch's size, so a point the grid steps over is found: on the ellipsoid (1.5, 1, 0.7) the umbilics at sin v = √((b² − c²)/(a² − c²)), where both curvatures are −ac/b³ = −1.05, fall between the columns of a 24-column grid, and the probe placed there reads an umbilic. Moving it asks Go for the point alone, over the study already computed (`SurfaceProbeOnly`). The plots still read the grid's samples on the nearest row or column, with the probe marked at its own place among them. A mirror and its light keep to their grids. Native tests check, between samples, a sphere's κ = −1/R and umbilic everywhere; an ellipsoid's K = 1/(a²b²c²(x²/a⁴ + y²/b⁴ + z²/c⁴)²), normal along the gradient and centers X + n/κ; the umbilic the grid misses; the offset's κᵢ/(1 − dκᵢ) and the patch's centers; the focal sheets' points, feet and normals eᵢ, on the nearest sample's side; that the point at every tested sample of an ellipsoid, a torus and a monkey saddle, with offsets, matches the grid there exactly; and that the probe alone matches the whole study's, its centers clipped against the patch's size, not the drawing's.

A surface built on a curve that Go evaluates at any parameter (a torus knot, a harmonic or a custom curve, not a trajectory or a pursuit) stands between samples too, its checkbox naming its own parameters (**At any t and θ − θ₀**, or **At any t and u**). The probe stands at `t = tMin·(1 − p) + tMax·p` and, across, at the turn `θ − θ₀ = 2πq` round a canal's contact circle, or at `u = (1 − q)·u₀ + q·u₁` along a framed ribbon's cross-line (−w to w) or a ruled surface's ruling (0 to 1). A tangent developable's share q runs through its 24 columns ±L·k/12 at an even pace between each two, so it crosses its edge of regression u = 0, which is singular, halfway. Go evaluates the curve, its derivatives and what the surface needs at t: the sphere's radius and the rotation-minimizing frame carried from the sample before t by one step of the transport the samples take (see [frames](#spatial-frames-offsets-and-ribbons)) for a canal, the frame carried likewise for a framed ribbon, the partner b(φ(t)) for a ruled surface. The point is described by the function that describes each grid sample (`canalAt`, `ruledAt`, engine3/surfacepoint.go), so at a sample it reads that sample to the bit, and centers are judged against the study's radius, as on the grid. A closed surface's last row repeats its first, and its point at the end of the domain is its point at the start, so a probe moved once round it loops. The point also carries the line the probe draws through it, which the grid draws from its own row: the contact circle, or the ruling from end to end. On the hyperboloid x² + z² − y²/c² = 1 ruled from s = −0.6 to s = 1.4 along (cos t − s sin t, cs, −sin t − s cos t), the throat s = 0 lies at u = 0.3 of each ruling, between the columns of a 25-column grid; there κ = 1/c² along the meridian and −1 round the throat, and K = −1/c², most negative along the ruling (**Round a hyperboloid's throat**). Native tests check the point at every column of six rows of eleven surfaces against the grid exactly, its convergence on a sample as it nears one, a torus's −1/r and K = (ρ − 2)/(r²ρ), the hyperboloid's closed-form K and its extremum on the throat, a helix developable's |κ| = b/(a|u|) and its singular edge, the helicoid's ±b/(b² + ρ²), the line through the point, refusals, and that a closed surface's ends read alike.

**Canals.** A canal is X = c + Rq, where c(s) is the curve in arc length s, R(s) the sphere radius, and q = −Ṙ T + σ e the unit normal, with σ = √(1 − Ṙ²) and e a unit vector normal to T, carried by the meridians' frame. Its two principal directions follow its two lines of curvature:

- **Around the contact circle**, κ = −1/R: every normal along the circle passes through the sphere's center c, so this branch's center is the point of the curve, and its normal section is a great circle of the sphere.
- **Across the circle**, the direction w is X_s with its component along the circle's tangent T × e removed, since principal directions are orthogonal, and κ = −q_s·w / w·w. With bent = κN the curve's curvature vector, q_s = −(R̈ + σ bent·e) T − Ṙ bent − (ṘR̈/σ) e. The frame's own turning about T moves e along T × e, which w leaves out. Ṙ = R′/v and R̈ = (R″ − Ṙ v′)/v² convert the profile's t-derivatives to arc length, with v = |c′| and v′ = T·c″, so the result does not depend on the curve's speed.

On a tube (Ṙ = 0) the second reduces to κ = κ_e/(1 − Rκ_e) with κ_e = bent·e, which on a torus's outer equator is −1/(a + R). On a straight curve the canal is a surface of revolution and κ = R̈/(1 − Ṙ² − RR̈). A canal has no umbilic at a regular point: with n = (X − c)/R, dn(w) = (w − T − Ṙq)/R, which equals w/R only where T = −Ṙq, that is where the contact circle has collapsed. A collapsed circle (|Ṙ| = 1, a cone's apex) and a cuspidal edge (w = 0, as where a tube is wider than the curve's radius of curvature) are singular. Where R̈ or c″ is numerically unstable the curvature across the circle is unknown, while the circle's own curvature and both directions are still given.

The canal's diagnostic grid is independent of its mesh: regular rows along t (every ⌈n/480⌉-th sample and the last, so at most 481 rows) and 24 turns around each circle from θ₀, as the meridians are measured (with any twist). A closed canal's last row is its first and is counted once. The probe snaps to this grid, so moving it never recomputes; more samples give finer steps, up to 481 rows. A row's t is reported as min + (max − min)·(i/n), as the curve probe's is.

**Accuracy.** Patch curvatures come from exact derivatives. A canal's come from the curve's and profile's own derivatives: analytic for knots and harmonic sums, otherwise the evaluator's five-point differences. On **Beads that lose their envelope** the curvature across each circle agrees with the closed form for a surface of revolution to a relative 1.2·10⁻⁹. On a canal of varying radius around a helix, the normal curvature of the drawn meridians, from second differences of their points, agrees with Euler's formula κₙ = κ₀cos²α + κ₁sin²α, and the error of that estimate falls fourfold per doubling of the samples (1.9·10⁻³, 4.7·10⁻⁴, 1.2·10⁻⁴ at 480, 960 and 1920). The readout gives four significant figures.

**Ruled surfaces.** The tangent developable, the framed ribbon and the ruled surface are all S(t, u) = a(t) + u d(t), straight along u, so S_uu = 0. With E, F, G and L, M the first and second fundamental forms (N = S_uu·n = 0), K = −M²/(EG − F²) ≤ 0: none is ever positively curved, and K = 0 exactly where M = S_tu·n = d′·n vanishes, which is det(a′, d, d′) = 0, the ruled construction's developability test. Each surface's curvatures come from its own derivatives, never from its drawn polyline, with the drawing's normal:

- **Tangent developable**, r + uT for |u| ≤ L, written r + w r′ with w = u/|r′| (the same points), so its second derivatives need only r‴. Its drawn normal is the binormal B where u > 0 and −B where u < 0, the reverse of S_t × S_u on both sides. In arc length S_s = T + uκN and S_u = T, so E = 1 + u²κ², F = G = 1, EG − F² = u²κ², and with that normal S_ss·n = |u|κτ and S_su·n = κN·n = 0. The ruling is therefore a principal direction with κ = 0 (center at infinity), and across it, along N, **κ = τ/(κ|u|)**, positive for a right-handed curve. K = 0: the ribbon is developable. The branches are named by line: **κ along the ruling** and **κ across it**. A planar curve gives a flat umbilic, both curvatures zero. At u = 0, the edge of regression along the curve, the sheets meet in a cusp and the surface has no normal; the grid leaves it out. A sample without the drawing's binormal, where r′ × r″ vanishes, is singular along its whole ruling; where r″ itself is unknown, its tangent plane is unknown instead.
- **Framed ribbon**, r + uD for |u| ≤ w, from how the frame turns rather than from its transported samples. In arc length, with bent = κN the curvature vector and P = T × D, D turns about T at a rate Ω and toward T only as the curve bends: D_s = −(bent·D)T + ΩP and P_s = −(bent·P)T − ΩD, so D_ss = −(bent_s·D + 2Ω bent·P)T − (bent·D)bent + Ω_s P − Ω²D. A rotation-minimizing frame has Ω = (2π·Twist + Correction)/L, constant; a Frenet frame turns with the torsion, Ω = τ + 2π·Twist/L and Ω_s = τ_s. The drawn normal is D × S_t. Then F = 0, G = 1, EG − F² = |S_t|² = |r′|²((1 − u bent·D)² + u²Ω²) and M² = Ω²|r′|²/((1 − u bent·D)² + u²Ω²), so **K = −Ω²/((1 − u bent·D)² + u²Ω²)²**: K = −Ω² at the spine, and an untwisted rotation-minimizing ribbon (Ω = 0) is developable. On a helix of radius a and pitch b, a ribbon along the principal normal is the helicoid ((a − u) cos t, (a − u) sin t, bt), a minimal surface with κ = ±b/(b² + ρ²), ρ = a − u; a Frenet ribbon with θ = 0 gives it, as does a rotation-minimizing one started along N and twisted at the torsion. Branches are numbered κ₁ ≥ κ₂.
- **Ruled surface**, (1 − u)a(t) + u b(φ(t)) for 0 ≤ u ≤ 1, with S_tt = (1 − u)a″ + u m² b″(φ). The normal is S_t × S_u, as drawn, and branches are numbered κ₁ ≥ κ₂. Coincident rulings, and points where S_t × S_u is within the drawing's guard (10⁻⁶ of |S_t||S_u|), are singular. Rings turned against each other span the hyperboloid x² + y² − z²sin²(δ/2) = cos²(δ/2); half-turn chords of the helix (2 cos t, 2 sin t, t/3) cross its axis at u = 1/2 with K = −9 and H = −π/4 at every t.

τ_s, where a Frenet ribbon needs it, is exact for knots and harmonic sums from their analytic r⁗: with b = r′ × r″, τ = b·r‴/|b|² and b′ = r′ × r‴, so τ′ = b·r⁗/|b|² − 2(b·r‴)(b·(r′ × r‴))/|b|⁴. Otherwise τ is differenced from the curve probe's r‴ at two steps, 10⁻³ and 5·10⁻⁴ of the domain, and the curvature is unknown, counted, where they disagree. At the two ends of an expression curve's domain the one-sided r‴ shifts τ enough that they do, so a Frenet ribbon's first and last rows read unknown there. Each ruled surface's grid has its rows along t as the canal's (at most 481) and its columns along the ruling: 24 on a developable, at u = ±L·k/12 for k = 1…12; 25 across a framed ribbon, from −w to w; 25 along a ruled surface, from 0 to 1. A closed surface's last row is its first and is counted once. A framed ribbon is probed only while it has a width.

**Accuracy of the ruled surfaces.** On a knot, where r‴ is analytic, a developable's κ across each ruling agrees with τ/(κ|u|), from the curve probe's own κ and τ, to a relative 10⁻⁹, and a Frenet ribbon's K at the spine with −(τ + 2π·Twist/L)² to 10⁻⁶. On expression curves, whose r‴ is differenced, the helix closed forms hold to a relative 5·10⁻⁵ inside the domain, less at its ends. A Frenet ribbon there needs τ′ differenced from a differenced r‴, and is the least accurate: on the trefoil written as expressions it agrees with the analytic knot's ribbon to 5.8·10⁻³ of the larger curvature, and 350 of its 12,025 points, around the torsion's sharp peaks, are unknown. The quadrics between rings agree with their level sets' K and H to 10⁻⁹. Euler's formula against the drawn surface's normal curvature along t, from second differences of its straight rulings' points, converges fourfold per doubling of the samples: 2.4·10⁻⁴, 6.0·10⁻⁵ and 1.5·10⁻⁵ on a knot's developable at 600, 1200 and 2400 samples; 5.2·10⁻³, 1.3·10⁻³ and 3.2·10⁻⁴ on a knot's Frenet ribbon, whose torsion peaks sharply; 1.5·10⁻⁴, 3.7·10⁻⁵ and 9.2·10⁻⁶ on a twisted rotation-minimizing ribbon around a harmonic curve; and 1.3·10⁻⁴, 3.2·10⁻⁵ and 8.0·10⁻⁶ on a loom between a ring and a rippled thread. The readout shows a curvature within 10⁻¹² of the other as 0, as along a developable's ruling, where it is exactly 0, and the plots draw variation within 10⁻⁴ of the largest curvature on the plotted line as constant, so that rounding is not drawn as geometry.

**Plots.** Each plot draws one principal curvature at the grid's own samples on one line through the probe's point, and marks the probe's sample. **Plots** chooses the line: along the probe's column, over the rows (u, or t on a surface built on a curve), which is the default; along its row, over the columns (v, a canal's θ − θ₀, or a ruled surface's u); or both, the rows' pair first. A canal's row is periodic, so its plot repeats its first column at the end and spans the full turn, back to θ₀; every other line runs from its first sample to its last. Each line is scaled on its own: variation within 10⁻⁴ of the largest |κ| on that line, on either branch, is drawn as constant, and values beyond the outer Tukey fences are pinned, as on the curve probe. A gap, a singular point or an unknown curvature breaks the line rather than being joined across. The values are the readout's own, from the same diagnostics, so nothing new is computed. Across a helix's tangent ribbon, for instance, κ along the ruling is 0 at every u and κ across it is 1/(6|u|), largest beside the edge of regression; around a canal of revolution's contact circle both curvatures are constant.

Moving the probe along a surface as an animation shows row round(p·m) of m at progress p, in the probe's column, so it starts at the first u or t and ends at the last exactly. While parameters vary, the surface probe stays at its row and column fractions, stays at its own parameters, or moves along the rows, as [the curve probe does](#spatial-curvature-torsion-and-the-parameter-probe); held at its own parameters, it snaps to the nearest row and column of each frame. Between grid samples, it moves instead through every u (or t), at u = uMin·(1 − p) + uMax·p in its own v, and while parameters vary Go places it in each frame at the same fractions of that frame's domain, at exactly its own (u, v), absent from a frame whose domain leaves either out, or at the progress's share of u. A reveal draws it once the row after it is drawn, or on a surface built on a curve the sample after it.

## Spatial ruled surfaces and chord families

A ruled surface joins two curves by straight segments, which requires a stated correspondence between their points. The ruled construction pairs the curve a(t) with a partner at parameter `φ(t) = m t + δ` and draws `S(t, u) = (1 − u) a(t) + u b(φ(t))` for `0 ≤ u ≤ 1`. The rate m (within ±100) and shift δ (within ±10⁶) are input, never inferred from where the curves happen to lie.

**Partners.** A _chord_ partner is the curve itself, `b = a`, so every ruling is a chord. On a closed curve (a torus knot, or a harmonic curve over whole periods) φ wraps around the period. On an open curve a chord exists only while φ(t) stays in the domain; elsewhere the partner is missing and counted, never clamped to an endpoint. A _thread_ partner is a second curve `b(s) = (x(s), y(s), z(s))`, written in t with the bounded parser (without the shape parameter a), and differentiated by the same stencils as a custom curve. It has no domain of its own: it is evaluated wherever φ sends it, and a nonfinite value there is a gap.

**Gaps and closure.** The surface's breaks are the base's, plus every interval where the partner is missing or jumps. A jump uses the same test as a custom curve's pole: the chord between neighboring partner points must be accounted for by the midpoint velocity `m b′(φ)`. The surface is closed when the curve is closed and the partner returns to its start (to 10⁻⁹ of the study's size), for example for a whole-number rate on a closed curve. Otherwise, on a closed curve, the distance between the partner's ends is reported and the seam is left open.

**Normals and degeneracy.** The surface is shaded with its true normal `S_t × S_u`, where `S_t = (1 − u) a′ + u m b′(φ)` and `S_u = d = b(φ) − a`. It is evaluated at five points along each ruling, and each interval is split into four strips, so a surface that is not developable does not have the constant normal of a tangent ribbon along its rulings. A ruling of length at most 10⁻⁹ of the study's size is _coincident_: the threads meet and the surface pinches there, so it is counted and has no faces. Where `S_t × S_u` otherwise vanishes to 10⁻⁶ of `|S_t||S_u|`, the surface is singular, as at a cone's apex or on an edge of regression. Such rulings are counted, and the faces around them are shaded with their own normals. Nothing is smoothed across. Self-intersections are drawn as they are, never trimmed or reported. In the harmonic loom preset, for example, the ripples on both rings tilt neighboring rulings through one another near the waist. A ruling at t = 0.698 meets the one at t = 1.422 at (−0.493, 0.598, 0.008), confirmed by Newton's method to 10⁻¹⁶, whereas the unrippled hyperboloid is embedded.

**Developability.** At regular points `S = a + u d` is developable exactly when `det(a′, d, d′) = 0`, with `d′ = m b′(φ) − a′`: the normal is then constant along each ruling. The construction reports the largest `|det(a′, d, d′)| / (|a′| |d| (|a′| + |d′|))` over the regular, non-coincident samples. This measure is independent of scale and parameterization. The surface is called developable when it is within 10⁻⁶. A cylinder (δ = 0 between parallel rings) and a cone (a constant thread) are developable. The hyperboloid between rings turned against each other is not.

**Checks.** Native tests verify that two unit rings at z = ∓1 with shift δ span the hyperboloid `x² + y² = cos²(δ/2) + z² sin²(δ/2)` at every mesh vertex (to 10⁻¹²), with normals parallel to `(x, y, −z sin²(δ/2))` and turning along each ruling; that δ = 0 gives a developable cylinder; that every vertex and ruling lies on the segment joining corresponding points; and that partners follow `b(m t + δ)`. They also cover chords of a torus knot at whole and fractional rates (closed, or with a reported gap) and chords stopping outside an open helix's domain, with the surface omitted there. Further tests cover zero-length chords, a cone singular at its apex with exact cone normals elsewhere, a single pinch, partner poles found by midpoint instability and by the jump test alone, rigid motion and reparameterization, framing a distant thread, and validation. The WebAssembly bridge test checks the hyperboloid, planar chords of a circle, collapse, and refusals.

## Spatial canal surfaces and tubes

A canal surface is the envelope of a one-parameter family of spheres: centers on the curve c(t), radius R(t) > 0. The canal construction takes `R(t) = R·ρ(t)`, with R a positive constant (at most 10⁵) and the profile ρ written in t with the bounded parser (without the shape parameter a), differentiated by the same stencils as a custom curve. A profile of 1 gives the tube of constant radius.

**Contact circles.** With `q = X − c`, a point X lies on the envelope when it satisfies both `|q|² = R²` (it is on the sphere) and `q · c′ = −RR′` (it is also on the neighboring spheres, the t-derivative of the first equation). With speed `v = |c′| > 0`, unit tangent T and `k = R′/v`, the solutions form the _contact circle_ centered at `c − R k T`, set back along the tangent, of radius `R√(1 − k²)` in the plane normal to T. The surface normal there is the sphere's, `q / R`: every mesh vertex is shaded with that exact normal. For a tube, k = 0, so the circle is the normal circle of radius R and the envelope is the familiar tube.

**Where the envelope fails.** The circle is real only while `|R′| ≤ v`. At `|R′| = v` (within 10⁻⁹) it _collapses_ to the point `c ∓ R T`, which is counted and kept. Where `|R′| > v` the spheres are nested inside one another and have no real envelope. Those samples are counted, have no circle, and every interval touching one is broken. The profile is also checked at each interval's midpoint against the midpoint speed, so an envelope that vanishes only between two samples still breaks that interval, and it is counted separately. A sample whose profile is not a positive, finite radius with a stable slope has no sphere and is counted. A _stationary center_, `c′ = 0`, is an invalid base sample: there `q · c′ = −RR′` has no solution when R′ ≠ 0 and is satisfied by the whole sphere when R′ = 0, so no circle is defined and the surface is left open. Nothing is joined across any of these gaps.

**Angle and closure.** The circle itself does not depend on any frame; only the angle around it does. It is carried by the rotation-minimizing frame of the framed construction (N₀, θ₀, twist, and the closed-loop seam; the Frenet diagnostic and the ribbon fields do not apply). Up to 12 _meridians_ `θ = θ₀ + 2πk/m` show that angle and its seam: on a closed loop they return turned by the frame's holonomy unless the correction is distributed. The mesh uses its own angular parameterization, the same frame with any holonomy always spread along a closed loop, so its first and last rings coincide and the surface closes whichever seam policy is chosen. The surface is closed when the curve is closed and the profile returns to its start with the same slope. Otherwise the distance between the first and last contact circles, which share an axis, is reported as the gap.

**Mesh and contact lines.** Regular mesh rings have 24 segments. Every displayed contact circle adds its sample as a mesh ring with 48 segments, using the mesh's angular frame and the very same vertices. Strips join coarse and fine rings in angular order without hanging edges. This keeps an inside view's flatter wall from hiding parts of its own contact circles. The regular rows remain every ⌈n/480⌉ samples, plus endpoints and rings beside gaps; displayed circles add at most the requested line count. Refined rings keep 24 segments and the existing added-vertex budget. Contact circles remain circles in the same planes with the same centers and radii; their polygonal phase follows the mesh's distributed holonomy rather than the meridians' angle, twist, and optional seam.

**Folds.** The surface is not trimmed, not a collision-free tube, and not the boundary of a union of balls. A tube wider than the radius of curvature, `Rκ > 1`, folds back through itself on the inner side of each bend. A varying radius can fold across a cusped edge where the contact circles run backward along the curve, as they do beside each collapse. A sample is counted as folded when, at one of 24 diagnostic angles, `(X_θ × X_t) · q/R < −10⁻⁶ |X_t|`. Here X_θ is exact and X_t is a second-order difference along that angle within an unbroken run. The sign is positive on a regular canal surface. Distant parts of the surface that pass through each other are drawn, not reported.

**Mesh bounds.** Each ring stores its 25 or 49 vertices once, including its closing point, and neighboring strips share them. Without surface refinement, every ring is on one of the n + 1 samples. Regular rows use a stride of ⌈n/480⌉, displayed circles add at most the requested line count, and rings beside gaps are always retained. Surface refinement keeps the existing 131,072 added-vertex budget. Automatic framing includes the drawn circle vertices, so aligning their polygonal phase can shift it slightly: the preset comparisons change the framing radius by less than 0.02%. Curve, frame, and surface diagnostics are unchanged.

**Checks.** Native tests verify:

- the torus `(√(x² + y²) − 2)² + z² = R²` around a circle, with exact normals, and a cylinder around a line;
- both envelope equations at every meridian, circle, and mesh point of a helix with a varying radius (to 10⁻¹² and 10⁻⁹), with vertex normals `q/R`;
- second-order convergence of the finite-difference surface tangent to orthogonality with q (3.3·10⁻³, 8.3·10⁻⁴, 2.1·10⁻⁴ at 240, 480, 960 samples);
- the 30° cone of `R = t/2` along a unit-speed line and the collapse of every circle to the apex for `R = t`;
- the samples without an envelope where `|R′| > v`, against the analytic count;
- an envelope that vanishes only at an interval's midpoint;
- a helix tube folded at every sample just beyond `1/κ` and at none just below;
- closure, the gap of a drifting profile, the meridians' seam with the mesh still closed, and the distributed correction;
- stationary centers and nonpositive radii, and mesh rings beside every gap at 2400 samples;
- every displayed contact-circle segment is a mesh edge in both tunnel presets with surface refinement on and off; mixed-resolution cylinder strips have consistent winding and no open interior edges, and dense surface refinement stays within its vertex budget;
- rigid motion and reparameterization, framing, and validation.

Mutations that drop the midpoint check, the mesh's holonomy correction, the rings beside gaps, or the fold sign each fail. The WebAssembly bridge test checks the torus, a profile whose envelope vanishes, and refusals.

## Spatial vector-field trajectories

A spatial vector field is the first-order system `r′ = V(x, y, z, t)`, written as three expressions for dx/dt, dy/dt and dz/dt in x, y, z, t and the shape parameter a. From 1–12 seeds (coordinates within ±10⁵), each trajectory starts at `t = t₀`, the interval start, and runs to the interval end. Trajectories are independent: one stopping never stops another. The first seed's trajectory is the base curve on which every construction is built; a construction is never repeated on the others. The construction **None** draws the curve alone, so trajectories can stand without a ribbon or surface.

**Integration.** Each trajectory has its own adaptive Dormand–Prince 5(4) steps, the stepper of the planar pursuit and trajectories (`engine/ode`, dimension-free). The local error of each step, the largest component of the embedded estimate, is held to `10⁻¹⁰` of the larger distance from the origin at the step's two ends, with a floor of `10⁻⁶ E` for escape radius E. Each step also moves at most `E/8` at the speed it starts with. A trajectory may attempt 50,000 steps. Positions between accepted steps are one step from the step's start, so a path is smooth within steps and continuous across them.

**Termination events.** A trajectory stops for exactly one reason, reported with its time, its last known position and its accepted steps:

- _end_: it reached the interval end;
- _escape_: an accepted step left the sphere `|r| = E` about the origin, and bisection of that step locates the last moment inside it. A seed outside the sphere escapes at t₀ with no position;
- _singular_: the field is not finite, or the steps shrink below `10⁻¹²` of the interval, as at a finite-time blow-up. A field that is not finite at the seed stops there at t₀;
- _exhausted_: the step budget ran out.

Samples after a trajectory's end are gaps, never joined or extrapolated. A seed is _resting_ when the field does not read t and its speed at the seed is below `10⁻⁹`; such a trajectory stays put. A field that reads t is never called resting, since vanishing at t₀ does not keep it still.

**One time on every path.** Every trajectory is sampled at the same evenly spaced times as the base, so a sample index is one physical time on every path, whatever their adaptive steps. Reveal slices every path at the same index, and an early stop is marked only once the reveal passes it.

**Derivatives from the field.** The base's velocity is the field itself, `r′ = V(r, t)`. Its acceleration `r″ = (∂V/∂r)V + ∂V/∂t` is the central difference of V along the flow, `[V(r + hV, t + h) − V(r − hV, t − h)]/2h`, at `h = 10⁻⁴` and `5·10⁻⁵` of the interval. It is discarded, as for custom curves, when the two disagree. Nothing is differentiated from the integrated positions, whose derivative is continuous only to the integration tolerance across steps. For a quadratic field such as Lorenz's or Rössler's, the difference is exact up to rounding. Tangent developables, involutes, projections, inversions, frames, ruled surfaces and canal surfaces then use this evaluator as they would any curve. A sample whose speed is below `10⁻⁹ L/Δ` has no tangent and is invalid, as at an equilibrium, with the base trajectory's size L and time span Δ as in [scale and translation](#spatial-scale-and-translation).

**Field directions.** At each representative sample, every trajectory carries the field's value there. The drawing shows its direction only, as a stroke of one length (7% of the fitted radius), never its speed. The paths are framed as their own families under every construction; the direction strokes are not.

**Curated conventions.** The presets use Lorenz's equations `(σ(y − x), x(ρ − z) − y, xy − βz)` with σ = 10, ρ = a = 28, β = 8/3, and Rössler's `(−y − z, x + ay, b + z(x − c))` with a = b = 0.2, c = 5.7. Tests check the coefficients through their equilibria: Lorenz's origin and `C± = (±√(β(ρ − 1)), ±√(β(ρ − 1)), ρ − 1)`, and Rössler's `x = (c − √(c² − 4ab))/2, y = −x/a, z = x/a`, all with speed below `10⁻¹²` and reported as resting. Chaotic trajectories separate from rounding differences. The checks are therefore aggregate: bounded boxes, repeated switches between Lorenz's lobes and Rössler's spikes, and identical results from one build. Long paths are not compared bit for bit. Other coefficients need not give chaos, and nothing claims they do.

**Workload.** At most 12 × 50,000 attempted steps of seven field evaluations. Twelve Lorenz seeds at 2400 samples over t ∈ [0, 100] took about 0.1 s natively; 3 MB of JSON alone, 19 MB under a tube.

**Checks.** Native tests verify:

- helices `(ρ cos(φ + t), ρ sin(φ + t), z₀ + at)` of the rising vortex `(−y, x, a)` at every sample of every seed, to 10⁻⁸, with seed 1 exactly the base;
- velocity and acceleration of the base against the helix and a timed field `(cos t, sin t, 1)`;
- the helix's exact unit tangent in the developable's rulings and its binormal as the sheet normal, with every construction running on a trajectory;
- straight lines of a constant field, and escape from radial growth at `t = ln(E/|r₀|)` (to 10⁻⁹) on the sphere, with no samples after it and none for a seed outside;
- the finite-time blow-up of `ẋ = 1/(1 − x)` at t = 1/2 (to 10⁻⁶), a field not finite at the seed, and an exhausted budget;
- Lorenz's and Rössler's equilibria and aggregate behavior, and determinism;
- error falling in proportion to the tolerance (2.5·10⁻³, 2.3·10⁻⁵, 2.3·10⁻⁷, 2.3·10⁻⁹ over ten helix turns at 10⁻⁵ to 10⁻¹¹), with more steps;
- translation of the field and seeds, a resting base seed (alone it is drawn, under a construction it is refused), and validation.

Mutations that drop the escape bisection or the timed-field rule for resting seeds each fail. The WebAssembly bridge test checks the helices, an escape time, a timed field and refusals.

## Spatial cyclic pursuit
