## Tesseract projections and sections

The independent `engine4` package models [−1, 1]⁴ with genuine four-dimensional rotations, linear and perspective projections, exact cell-edge intersections with w = h, and stereographic projections after radial normalization onto S³. See [Tesseract studies](tesseracts.md#mathematics-and-numerical-limits) for rotation order, formulas, analytic clipping, tolerance conventions, workload bounds, and numerical checks. The planar and spatial curve engines retain their existing definitions.

## Four-dimensional curved solids

The independent `engine4` evaluator supports exact axis-aligned sections of a centered 4-ball and the circular tubular solid `(sqrt(x²+y²)−R)²+z²+w² ≤ r²`, with `0 < r < R`. The former has 3-ball sections; the latter has solid-torus sections with fixed core radius R. Their boundary radii are `sqrt(R²−h²)` and `sqrt(r²−h²)`. At support tangency they collapse respectively to a point and a core circle, then become empty. Representative boundary circles are polylines; they do not reduce the section's dimension to one or describe its entire interior. [The 4D study](tesseracts.md#exact-curved-sections) defines formulas, tangency tolerances, source identities, limits and motion. General tilted curved sections and implicit 4D fields are outside this evaluator.

## Localized four-dimensional lift

For the fixed source curves C(t), the embedding is (C(t), H(C(t))), where H(p) = A(1−|p−c|²/L²)² inside |p−c| < L and zero outside. This C¹ bump preserves connectivity, while generally changing lengths. A slab of fixed half-thickness ε = 0.02 around the embedding intersects w = 0 exactly where H(p) ≤ ε. When A > ε, the absent region is the open ball of radius L sqrt(1−sqrt(ε/A)); otherwise every source point is present. Source/sphere crossings are solved analytically before subdivision. The explanatory view rotates xw then drops w; that transform never changes reference membership. [The localized lift guide](tesseracts.md#localized-thread-lift) documents sources, interval tolerances, bounds, identities and motion. This is an embedding demonstration with no physical tension or flow model.

## Four-dimensional shell bypass

For the closed embedded shell `K = {(p,w): a ≤ |p| ≤ b, |w| ≤ ε}`, a three-leg route from `(p_out,0)` through `(p_out,H)` and `(0,H)` to the origin clears K when H > ε and D = |p_out| > b. Its distance to K is `min(D−b,a,H−ε)`. Equality contacts the closed shell; a lower route crosses it. By contrast, the radial shell `K₄ = {q: a ≤ |q| ≤ b}` obstructs every continuous outside-to-origin path by the intermediate value theorem applied to |q|. Analytic sphere/slab events partition complete segments before membership classification, independently of the displayed prefix or boundary subdivisions. The XYZ shadow discards w. The shadow of K is the 3D shell a ≤ |p| ≤ b, while the shadow of K₄ is the filled ball |p| ≤ b, since (p,0) with |p| < a lifts to (p,w) with a² ≤ |p|²+w² ≤ b². The companion (|p|,w) diagram retains w at equal scale. Both obstacles depend only on (|p|,w), and each route leg is a straight segment in that plane, so the diagram represents membership exactly. Points `(p,w₁)` and `(p,w₂)` have identical XYZ shadows but separation `|w₂−w₁|` in R⁴. [The shell bypass guide](tesseracts.md#shell-bypass) specifies source identities, tolerances, bounds, motion and diagnostic limitations.

## Spherical ring weaves

On the unit 3-sphere, `q(u,v,α) = (cos α cos u, cos α sin u, sin α cos v, sin α sin v)` traces a torus for `0 < α < π/2` and collapses to one circle at either endpoint. With `z₁ = x+iy` and `z₂ = z+iw`, the Hopf fibers `(e^{it}z₁, e^{it}z₂)` are great circles on which `H(z₁,z₂) = (2 Re z₁z̄₂, 2 Im z₁z̄₂, |z₁|²−|z₂|²)` is constant. Any two distinct fibers link once. After a 4D rotation every source is still a circle `c + a cos t + b sin t`, and stereographic projection `q_xyz/(1−q_w)` maps it to a circle, or to a line through the pole. The window `|P| ≤ C` is `q_w ≤ (C²−1)/(C²+1)`, and `q_w(t)` is a constant plus one sinusoid, so each circle's window crossings are found in closed form before sampling. [The spherical weave guide](tesseracts.md#spherical-ring-weave) documents tolerances, collapsed endpoints, identities, bounds and motion. These are surfaces and curves on S³, not four-dimensional solids.
